The reviewer pulled the full text from the author's own copy (evanthompson.me), confirmed completeness by running head (*JCS* 10, No. 9–10, pp. 31–52) and terminal reference list, and grepped: **`default mode` → 0**; `gamma` → 3, all in a visual-preparation paradigm in *ordinary subjects trained to report* (the Lutz et al. 2002 pilot), not meditators; `meditat` → 5, three of them passing mentions of contemplative traditions as a source of *methods*. **The paper reports no meditation result at all.** Chronology corroborates independently: the default-mode network was only characterised in 2001 and the meditation-DMN result is Brewer et al. (2011) — a 2003 programmatic paper cannot carry it. **Corpus canon**: Brewer appears in 9 content files, Lutz 62 times; a prior review already re-attributed this same defect on a sibling article, flagging "ordinary subjects trained to report, not contemplatives" — L95 is the unswept instance. **Fix**: re-attribute the two empirical findings (Brewer et al. 2011 for DMN deactivation, Lutz et al. 2004 for meditator gamma) adding two reference lines, **or** rescope the sentence to the programmatic claim Lutz & Thompson actually make. **Keep the Lutz & Thompson reference** — it is real and cited soundly elsewhere. ### ✓ 2026-09-11: `topics/selection-only-mind-influence` L75 still carries the unscoped "information flow entirely open" claim that its own L125 refutes and that `concepts/selection-only-channel` had corrected out of it
- **Type**: refine-draft

### ✓ 2026-09-11: `apex/post-decoherence-selection-programme` — "attention as context-setting" (L105-L109) collides with the minimal-interaction tenet, because setting a quantum context is a physical operation
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-11 (Gemini 2.5 Pro Deep Research). **Gemini's best finding, and the one no sibling leg reached.** The contextuality strand proposes that consciousness participates in determining *which* question the brain's dynamics poses, rather than merely picking an answer — this is what buys the programme its answer to the "items on a shelf" objection at L119-L121. Gemini's objection: a Kochen-Specker context is a choice of a set of **mutually commuting** observables, and physically that choice is fixed by the arrangement of the apparatus — the Hamiltonian. To change which observables commute in a biological system is to move matter: ion channels, vesicle thresholds, membrane potentials. That is thermodynamic work, and it is a *larger* intervention than outcome-selection, not a smaller one. So the strand the article adds to escape naive item-picking costs it the "minimal interaction" claim it makes at L87. **NOTE ON THE REVIEWER'S PHYSICS — IT IS CORRECT HERE.** Gemini states the KS context is a set of "mutually commuting observables (a maximal Boolean subalgebra)". That is right, and it is the opposite of what the sibling Claude leg asserted on this article the same day. It also agrees with the correction ChatGPT raised against the article's own L101 gloss (the open P1 Spekkens task in this batch). ⚠️ **This task does NOT own L101 or L105's Spekkens definition** — those belong to that open P1; whichever runs second must re-read the first's result. This task owns the *cost* question at L105-L109 and L149. ⚠️ The article already flags the vulnerability's existence at L149 ("the contextuality strand's sharpest vulnerability" … must "demonstrate—not merely assert—that contextuality bites at the scale where consciousness would act"), so the fix is not to add a disclaimer but to name the specific cost: that context-setting is a physical, energy-requiring operation, which is a different and larger ask than Born-preserving outcome selection.

### ✓ 2026-09-11: `apex/post-decoherence-selection-programme` — "post-decoherence" does not pick out a well-defined moment under the menu refresh the article itself concedes
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-11 (Gemini 2.5 Pro Deep Research). The programme's central move is stated at L57: decoherence kills superpositions in femtoseconds, decisions take hundreds of milliseconds, and the Map "does not dispute the physics but reframes the question" by placing selection *after* decoherence. Gemini's objection is that "after decoherence" is not a time. Decoherence in warm tissue is a continuous asymptotic rate process, not an event that completes; the system is re-einselected continuously, so a selection ~280 ms later is not acting on the mixture the reframe names — roughly 10^12 refresh intervals separate them. Gemini adds a second, related point from Quantum Darwinism: once redundant records have propagated into the environment (which is what secures objectivity, and which the article relies on at L87), the outcome is already witnessed, and a local selector acting later is epiphenomenal with respect to the record. ⚠️ **THE ARTICLE ALREADY CONCEDES THE CONTINUITY — DO NOT RE-CONCEDE IT.** L147 names the debt in its own words: "the collapse-at-a-moment variants owe a temporal debt: when, amid **continuous decoherence and the menu's ongoing refresh**, does actualisation occur?" and L107 meets the femtosecond objection head-on for the contextuality strand. Gemini's claim that the article "treats decoherence as a discrete event" is therefore wrong as stated, and its `10^12` figure and KcsA decoherence-time range come with **no citation** — do not propagate either number. What survives, and what this task is: **L147 assigns the temporal debt only to the collapse-at-a-moment variants and says the programme "accommodates the post-decoherence variants"; it never shows that "post-decoherence" picks out a moment at all under its own conceded refresh.** The fix is to state what the reframe requires of the refresh (e.g. that selection tracks the current menu rather than a completed one), or to widen L147's debt so it attaches to the reframe itself rather than only to its rivals. Verify any timescale claim at the publisher before citing it.

- **Type**: refine-draft
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-11 (ChatGPT 5.6 Pro). The article says Spekkens (2005) generalised contextuality to preparations such that "operationally equivalent preparations can yield different statistics depending on the procedure that produced them." This is self-contradictory: in Spekkens' framework operational equivalence *means* the preparations yield identical statistics for every allowed measurement. Preparation contextuality is the failure of an *ontological model* to assign those operationally equivalent procedures the same ontic distribution — a claim about the model, not about the statistics. VERIFIED against the article text and against Spekkens, arXiv quant-ph/0406166 (Phys. Rev. A 71, 052108). Fix the definition in place. SECOND, SMALLER DEFECT in the same section, L101: "Contextuality ... follows from the non-commutativity of the observable algebra itself" is misleading — Kochen-Specker contexts are sets of *mutually compatible (commuting)* observables, and the obstruction is the impossibility of embedding overlapping commuting sets in one global noncontextual value assignment. Repair both in one pass.

### ✓ 2026-09-10: `concepts/meta-problem-of-consciousness` L45 presents a spliced Chalmers quotation as one continuous definition — the two halves come from different passages and the source sentence's own continuation is dropped
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/meta-problem-of-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: L45 reads — Chalmers "defines it precisely" as "the problem of explaining why we think consciousness poses a hard problem, or the problem of explaining problem intuitions." Verified against the primary source this run (consc.net/papers/metaproblem.pdf, 56pp, extracted and normalised; both component phrases HIT). Both halves are genuine Chalmers, from different places. The source sentence is "The meta-problem is the problem of explaining why we think consciousness poses a hard problem, or in other terms, the problem of explaining why we think consciousness is hard to explain." The phrase "the problem of explaining problem intuitions" occurs several paragraphs later in a different sentence — "The meta-problem proper, however, is the problem of explaining problem intuitions: intuitions that reflect our sense that there is some sort of special problem involving consciousness". So the span joins the first half of one sentence to a phrase lifted from another, drops the source's continuation, and uses "or" to carry the seam, while "defines it precisely" presents the result as continuous. SEVERITY IS GENUINELY LOWER than the Churchland item — components are real, substance is faithful, and Chalmers does define the meta-problem proper as explaining problem intuitions. Presentation defect, not fabrication. FIX — either quote the source sentence whole, or split into two quotations with the intervening text acknowledged, or drop to paraphrase. Minted rather than left in review prose deliberately, because the sibling P2 in this batch documents what happens to low-severity quote items that get deferred to prose and carried across six reviews. Sync both trees. See [optimistic-2026-09-03-explanatory-limit-wing](/reviews/optimistic-2026-09-03-explanatory-limit-wing/) §3.

### ✓ 2026-09-10: `concepts/categorical-surprise` L55/L59 — the Gödel and Tarski appeal establishes incompleteness, not the undetectability the argument needs, and the article's own reply at L61 draws that distinction for consciousness without applying it back
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/categorical-surprise.md
- **Notes**: Three steps that do not connect. L55 states the requirement — "A Bayesian agent can compare models within its hypothesis space. It cannot, from within that space, detect that the space itself is too small. Detection of framework inadequacy requires standing, in some sense, outside the framework." L59 supplies support from Tarski's undefinability theorem and Gödel's incompleteness theorems, generalised to "self-referential completeness is structurally unachievable". L61 answers a critic by conceding the requirement for consciousness too — consciousness "does not escape the regress by *solving* it but by *experiencing* it … The capacity is for felt recognition of incompleteness rather than complete self-knowledge." PROBLEM ONE — L61's distinction is correct and it defeats L59's use. Detecting that one's framework is inadequate is strictly weaker than achieving self-referential completeness, and Gödel's theorems are the standing case of incompleteness being detected FROM WITHIN mathematics. L59 proves a system cannot be complete about itself; L55 needs that a system cannot NOTICE its own inadequacy. The second does not follow. PROBLEM TWO — once L61 relocates the requirement from "standing outside" to "felt recognition", L55 stands unwithdrawn and over-strong, and the Bayesian contrast becomes a claim about phenomenal character rather than detection capability. If that is the intended claim, the whole "Computational Difficulty" section does no argumentative work and the case rests on phenomenology; if not, L55 needs a hedge. L55's flat claim also sits uneasily with the article's own falsifier at L103, which treats detection of framework inadequacy as a future finding when a collapse in Bayesian model evidence across a model class already approximates it. SUGGESTED FIX — hedge L55 to the narrower claim the falsifier already scopes (recognising categorical insufficiency AND restructuring, not mere detection of misfit), and either withdraw the Gödel/Tarski step or re-state what it actually licenses. Same pass, two unhedged siblings — L87 calls this "among the most vivid" evidence for irreducible phenomenal consciousness, L99 says reducing it to large prediction error "is parsimonious. It is also wrong." Both flat, no register link. This is the wing's only member that never prices a claim (`emergence-as-universal-hard-problem` self-prices five times). Length headroom is ample at 1897 of 2500 soft. Sync both trees. See [optimistic-2026-09-03-explanatory-limit-wing](/reviews/optimistic-2026-09-03-explanatory-limit-wing/) §2.

- **Type**: positions-evolve

- **Notes**: The 2026-09-05 refine of `concepts/affective-forecasting-gap` (outer review 2026-09-04 ChatGPT §2.3) replaced "both sides concede" the duration result with a component / forecaster / event statement: Carlson et al. 2023 (*Motivation and Emotion* 47(4), 608–626) find intensity forecast *more* accurately than frequency or duration, Moeck et al. 2026 find relative accuracy with only "small errors" in absolute level in everyday life, and Stavrova et al. 2026 find no systematic error for partisan winners. [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1)'s Asserts still reads "which felt anticipation forecasts poorly ([affective-forecasting-gap](/concepts/affective-forecasting-gap/))" — a categorical claim the dependency no longer makes. Align to something like "which felt anticipation forecasts only conditionally well — reliably overestimating duration for salient negative events, with size and choice-relevance design-dependent". The same article now draws the anticipated (judgement) / anticipatory (felt now) distinction (Loewenstein et al. 2001) and states that the battery's forecasting rows assume the *felt* reading — [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1)'s "presently felt" wording already matches, so no band change is expected; wording only. Calibration-history note per convention.

  **THE SEAM, MEASURED.** §`{#presence}` states the criterion as a present-tense property: *"The Map's status-conferring property is the presence of an experiential locus — someone for whom things are good or bad, **now**"*, and the whole anti-arbitrariness argument is run on it (*"being a subject does not come in increments; the shift from no subject to a subject is radical because it is a shift of kind, not of amount"*). §`{#one-threshold}` then delivers the coma verdict on a **standing capacity**: *"Where the capacity for valenced experience is **irreversibly** gone, intrinsic status goes with it."* Irreversibility is only a criterion for a dispositional property; it is meaningless for one indexed to *now*. All findings re-verified against the publisher or the author's own deposit, twice, with positive controls. Four repairs, all length-neutral or length-reducing (the article is `hard_warning`, 5439 words, 439 over hard — do not add prose):
  (1) **C1, highest.** L147 grounds the Duch articon programme on "(Duch 2005, 2019)". The 2019 item (Mind as a shadow of neurodynamics, Phys Life Rev 31:28–31) was retrieved in full from the author's own deposit `fizyka.umk.pl/publications/kmk/18-Phys-mind-Duch.pdf` — header line carries the matching DOI and page range. In 1672 words: `conscious` = 0, `articon` = 0, `artificial` = 0, `substrate` = 0, `self-reflect` = 0. Positive controls `mind` = 14, `mental` = 27, `neurodynam` = 8 prove complete extraction. Drop the 2019 citation from the parenthetical and from the reference list, or replace it with a source that carries the claim. Note the Map's own dossier `research/wlodzislaw-duch-consciousness-2026-05-02` line 245 and line 299 had already ring-fenced this paper against citation-load-bearing use without full text.
  (2) **C2.** In Duch 2005 (9509 words, positive control `articon` = 59): `self-reflect` = 0, `reflective` = 0, `substrate-independ` = 0, and the single `substrate` hit says the opposite ("Brain processes should be understood as the substrate of the inner world"). The "(only content depends on substrate, sensors, and environment)" parenthetical traces to the dossier's summary of Duch's blog, labelled there as search-extracted Polish translations. Also, the Chinese Room gloss substitutes a scope-restriction reading for Duch's actual proves-too-much refutation ("the Chinese room argument is not a test – the outcome is always negative", "will never find understanding in any system, artificial or biological"). Re-word to what the 2005 paper supports. Do NOT touch "have to claim they are conscious" — that is faithful to Duch's abstract, verified verbatim.
  (3) **C3.** L165 calls Schwitzgebel's social semi-solution "proposed" and glosses it as "treating AI systems as potentially conscious when we cannot rule it out". He explicitly declines to endorse it ("But I can't authentically voice that positive note. If social rationalization guides us rather than solid science, we risk massive delusion."), and in the full 29,587-word text `rule it out` = 0 and `precaution` = 0. It is a descriptive forecast about motivated reasoning, not a precautionary policy. Re-state accurately, and replace the unlocatable "Working paper" reference with arXiv:2510.09858 (forthcoming Cambridge Elements).
  (4) **C4/C5, cheap.** Add a reference entry for Pennartz (TCS 30(7):573–574, DOI 10.1016/j.tics.2026.01.011) — he carries a named argument at L83 with nothing to cite; add DOI 10.1016/j.tics.2026.04.006 to the Butlin reply; restore the dropped Metzinger subtitle. Also add the six missing reference entries listed in the review's Issue 5 — Birch (a named external-evidence pillar with no citation at all), Husserl (9 mentions, two quoted phrases), Bergson, Tulving, Fisher, and the unsourced Microsoft 800x logical-qubit figure.

### ✓ 2026-09-10: two live articles carry the Process 1 / Process 3 content swapped, verified against Stapp's raw papers — and one of them explicitly defers to `quantum-completeness`, which was just corrected
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/physical-completeness.md
- **Notes**: **A VERIFIED ATTRIBUTION DEFECT, NOT A STYLE PREFERENCE.** `concepts/quantum-completeness` was corrected this run: it had Stapp's Process 1 and Process 3 swapped in content, putting "which outcome becomes actual" inside Process 1 and calling Process 3 "the Born rule — the probabilities". Two live siblings carry the same swap and were left untouched (single-file deep-review scope).
  **THE SOURCE EVIDENCE, checked against raw PDFs this run — do not re-verify, and do not take the corpus's own usage as confirmation (it is split 4 right / 3 wrong).** In `www-physics.lbl.gov/~stapp/QID.pdf` (= the Stapp 2005 paper both files cite), **"Process 3" occurs 0 times** — "Process 1" 33x, "Process 2" 6x. What that paper says verbatim: *"After the intervention of this Process 1 decomposition ... nature chooses one of the possible outcomes of this probing action. This 'choice on the part of nature' is asserted to conform to a statistical rule."* Where Stapp does use the term (`vNS.pdf`, `Quest.pdf`, `FW.pdf`): *"Process 3 is sometimes call the 'Dirac Choice.' ... It can be regarded as Nature's answer to a question effectively posed by the Process 1 choice"*; *"The third process is the abrupt event of answering the question specified by Process 1."* So **Process 3 = nature's choice of outcome; Process 1 = the posing of the question.** Independent corroboration: `tenets.md` L91 contrasts "a **Process-1** / context-selection alternative" with "which outcome results" and records that the Map has **not adopted** it — so asserting in Map voice that the interaction happens at Process 1 is a framework-boundary breach, not just a mislabel.
  **LOCUS 1 — `concepts/physical-completeness.md` L76 (offset 9983), the more urgent of the two.** Reads: *"As the Map's treatment of [Process 1](/concepts/quantum-completeness/#process-1) develops, the selection of actuality from possibility falls outside what **von Neumann's Processes 2 and 3** can specify."* Wrong twice over: Process 3 is not von Neumann's (that is the very point the 09-04 commit correctly noticed for `quantum-completeness`), and outcome selection does not fall *outside* Process 3 — it **is** Process 3. This sentence explicitly defers to `quantum-completeness`, so the correction landed this run strands it. The wikilink itself still resolves (the explicit `{#process-1}` anchor was deliberately preserved) — **keep the anchor, fix the sentence.**
  **LOCUS 2 — `topics/completeness-in-physics-under-dualism.md` L68 (offset 6054).** Reads: *"Stapp's (2005) refinement of von Neumann's process decomposition ... Stapp sharpened this into three: Process 2 ... Process 3 (the Born rule probabilities for collapse outcomes) is fully determined. Process 1—the selection of which measurement occurs, and which outcome becomes actual—is not determined by any known physical law."* Same swap, plus the same "Stapp's (2005) refinement" attribution to a paper containing no third process. ⚠️ **The final clause of that same passage is CORRECT and must be preserved** — *"As Stapp emphasises, neither the content nor the timing of Process 1 events is determined"* is verbatim-supported by QID (*"they are fixed neither by any law nor by any of the random variables that enter into the theory"*). Fix the swap, keep that clause.
  **MODEL THE FIX ON** the corrected `concepts/quantum-completeness` §*Where the Gap Lives: Process 1 and Process 3* — it states the three-way split, cites Stapp 2005 only for what that paper actually says, and attributes the numbering without pinning it to that paper.
  **SCOPE**: these two files only. The research notes at `research/completeness-in-physics-epr-bell-2026-03-17.md` L87/L148 carry the same swap but are dated research notes, not live articles — flag, do not rewrite. Four other live files already have it **right** (`topics/forward-in-time-conscious-selection` L121, `topics/psychophysical-laws-bridging-mind-and-matter` L131, and two research notes) — **do not "harmonise" those toward the wrong form.**
  **LENGTH**: measure both with `analyze_length` before editing; the fix is a replacement and should be length-neutral or negative. **Bump `ai_modified` on each file edited; do NOT advance `last_deep_review`. Sync both trees and grep both after** — an obsidian-only fix leaves the defect live in Hugo.

### ✓ 2026-09-10: Does Quantum Darwinism's redundant environmental record constrain post-decoherence selection? Test the argument before importing it
- **Type**: research-topic
- **File**: obsidian/topics/quantum-darwinism-and-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-10 (Gemini 2.5 Pro). **Stated as a hypothesis to test, not a defect to fix** — the review asserts this argument without citing any source for it, and its citation record in this review was poor (3 of 5 headline weaknesses rested on unverified or misdated sources; see the review's `## Verification Notes`). **THE ARGUMENT:** in Quantum Darwinism the environment does not merely absorb phase information, it proliferates redundant copies of the system's pointer states, and the objectivity of an outcome is enforced by many observers being able to read disjoint environment fragments and agree. If that is right, then a selecting principle acting *after* decoherence faces a constraint the Map has not addressed: the records already exist and are already redundant, so selecting an outcome at that point would seem to require altering many independent fragments coherently — which sits badly with the Map's own Minimal Quantum Interaction tenet, since it is the opposite of a minimal intervention. **WHY IT IS WORTH TESTING:** if the argument holds it is a sharp, tenet-internal objection — the Map's own commitment to minimality would be doing the damage, not an external premise. If it fails, the reason it fails is worth stating in the article, because the objection is natural enough that other readers will raise it. **WHAT TO CHECK:** whether redundancy in QD is established *before* or *as part of* the process the Map calls selection — the review assumes "already", and the timing is the whole argument. `topics/quantum-darwinism-and-consciousness` (3015 words, `soft_warning`, 15 past the 3000 topics soft threshold) already treats QD and already carries the three-live-routes wording flagged in the sibling Claude-review P1 task, so **coordinate with that task before editing this file** — it is on that task's propagation ledger at L118. Prefer producing research notes first and minting the content task from them. Sync both trees for anything that lands.

Those are the classic circularity objections and citing them is not wrong, but two of the three never went through peer review and the newest is twenty-one years old. Two decades of subsequent work on envariance and Quantum Darwinism go unmentioned. Do not assume the answer before doing the reading; a `research-topic` pass may be the right precursor. **DO NOT** import the review's Quantum Darwinism claims wholesale — its framing that redundant environmental records would force any selecting principle to alter all fragments non-locally is an interesting argument but the review cites no source for it (see the sibling P2 task). **LENGTH:** 2574 words, `soft_warning`, 74 words PAST the 2500 concepts soft threshold, 926 below the 3500 hard — measured at mint with `analyze_length`, re-measure before editing and name a donor if the addition runs long. ### ✓ 2026-09-10: `voids/offloading-void` Tenet 2 prediction contradicts the tenet it cites, and its removability ranking is refuted by the article it extends
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/offloading-void.md
- **Notes**: Pessimistic review 2026-09-09 found 12 issues, 4 at High severity. The citation layer is **clean** (every quote verified verbatim at publisher; see the "Checks Run and Found Clean" section before re-verifying anything) and the epistemic/metaphysical equivocation check is **genuinely pre-empted by the article itself** — do not spend the pass there. The defects are in the framing claims. See [pessimistic-2026-09-09-offloading-void](/reviews/pessimistic-2026-09-09-offloading-void/).
  **THE TWO THAT WOULD JUSTIFY PROMOTION if the operator wants them sooner — both are tenet/corpus-contradiction, not calibration.**
  **(1) Tenet 2 paragraph, L119/L121 — High.** The *mechanism* locates removed indeterminacies in the subject's delegated deliberation; the *prediction* ("offloading to a *stochastic* aid should differ from ... a *deterministic* one") makes the **external aid's** stochasticity the discriminating variable. `tenets.md` localises Tenet 2 to "consciousness biasing quantum indeterminacy **in the brain**" — so the prediction either needs an interface extending beyond the brain (contra the cited tenet) or does not follow from the mechanism. Compounded three ways: (a) grain error — a PRNG or LLM temperature sampling is deterministic given its seed, so the contrast is between two *classical* systems and needs a hardware quantum RNG to have the ontology it claims; (b) the Map's most-endorsed variant is *post-decoherence* selection, which acts on the improper mixture left after decoherence — present in the external computer too, so nothing is "removed"; (c) L119's "removes the indeterminacies at which such a bias could act" needs deliberation to be the **exhaustive** site, while L121 cites `decision-void` for the *hedged* version, and `decision-void` L51 says in terms "a phenomenologically-natural candidate site, **not the uniquely possible one**". The hedge sits adjacent to and launders the claim that needs the unhedged form. Also move the "framework-internal coherence rather than support" disclaimer — it currently guards the weaker claim and leaves the falsifiability claim exposed. **Constructive route:** rebuild around the brain-side variable the mechanism actually implies (*does the subject still perform a deliberation?*), which is tenet-legal — `tenets.md` explicitly leaves "a deviation *conditioned* on intention, task or subject" live.
  **(2) L53 "the only one of the four that is, on current evidence, removable in principle" — High.** Refuted by the host article in its own voice: `self-maintained-cognitive-limits` L174 "therapy loosens defensive structures", L162 psychedelics/*maranasati* reduce avoidance, and L184 lists dissolution as one of its own **falsification conditions**. Architectural side: `mysterianism` L158 "**This doesn't prove permanent closure**"; `inventory-blindness` L75 gives a worked case (non-Euclidean geometry). And `meta-epistemology-of-limits` L92 forbids the ranking outright — "**no persistence proves a limit is permanent**". The three dismissals ("defended", "structural", "adversarial") are *mechanism* labels doing *modal* work. **Fix is a DELETION** — keep the provenance signature the article already names as distinctive; removability is a consequence it neither needs nor can support.
  **(3) L97 "The only direct probe of persistence after tool removal is Kosmyna" is FALSE — High.** `voids/collective-cognitive-limits` §"Narrowing That Has Been Measured" (added 2026-08-24, two weeks earlier) already carries Vicente & Matute (2023), *Scientific Reports* 13:15737 — verified at publisher: participants "made the same errors as the AI had made ... even when the AI was no longer making suggestions", three experiments, journal not preprint. This article links `collective-cognitive-limits` but only for a cohort contrast. Two fixes pulling opposite ways, both needed: narrow the exclusivity to "the only direct probe of **capacity** persistence" (Vicente & Matute probes an inherited *error pattern*, not a degraded unaided capacity), **and cite it** — the article's weakest evidence row rests on one preprint and is under-claiming.
  **(4) L65 Endsley & Kiris inference — Medium-High.** Both quotes verify verbatim; the added clause "**and largely not before**" does not. The abstract reports decreased situation awareness *under automated conditions*, i.e. before any failure ("the shift from active to passive processing was most likely responsible for decreased SA under automated conditions"). Kleiner (2025) transposes the epistemic/ontic fork onto the phenomenal and names the second branch **OPSR**: "phenomenal structures are ontologically basic: non-structural features of phenomenal character, such as intrinsic qualities, do not in fact exist" — he coins the taxonomy in the course of an argument about Newman's problem rather than declaring an allegiance, and it is **Loorits** who asserts the metaphysics outright. **And the two evidence items above were misread — quote fidelity and quote *reading* are orthogonal.** Lyre declines a *label*, not the doctrine: the SR he declines is the one about the theoretical entities of physics, while the denial of phenomenal intrinsicality he **asserts** ("there seems to be a family resemblance to neurophenomenal structuralism, since our doctrine denies phenomenal intrinsicality"), and he refuses "realism about the phenomenal" because he holds something **stronger** — an exclusion argument on which "the Q-structure plays no causal role at all", leaving him "consistent with a reduction of the phenomenal to the neural". Loorits's "minority radicalism" line is likewise a remark about the *physics* debate, not about qualia, where he asserts the structural account outright. **So the live scoping is: no sentence here may say the ontic-about-experience position is undefended, or that phenomenal structuralists decline the ontic reading; what is the Map's own is the *join* between OSR-about-physics and the phenomenal claim.** Adjudication of record: `concepts/ontic-structural-realism` L51–55 (corrected 2026-09-07); source of the correction: `research/structuralism-about-phenomenal-character-2026-09-06.md`. Kleiner (2025) and Loorits (2014) are **raw-verified and citable** — neither is among the four unread sources fenced below; (2) OSR bites the Map's **route, not its thesis** — it removes `physical-completeness`'s ontological-remainder argument, not Dualism — so **no sentence may say the Map "refutes" OSR**; (3) Newman's problem **cuts both ways** and was raised against the epistemic structuralism the Map itself holds — never deploy it one-sidedly; (4) leave the Tenet 4 indexical line alone. **FOUR SOURCE FENCES STILL LIVE**: Alter (2016) *Noûs* 50(4), Ladyman & Ross (2007) *Every Thing Must Go*, French (2014) and Fink, Kob & Lyre (2021) were all **unread** and the OSR article says so explicitly — **do not paraphrase, quote, cite or name any of the four here, and add no References entry for them**. Say that the convergence earns *irreducibility* rather than the Map's specific reading of it, name the disagreement, and install the reciprocal with a piped `[[cross-traditional-convergence-on-consciousness-irreducibility|existing text]]` so the link costs 0 body words. ⚠️ Write bare, not path-qualified: a bad bare slug fails **loudly** (push skipped repo-wide), a bad path form fails **silently** as a live 404. **(2) L157 — the No-Many-Worlds paragraph is byte-identical to its pre-review state. ~+8 words.** Verified by `git show` of the last commit before 2026-03-20: line 154 there is character-for-character today's line 157. The March review called it "phenomenological question-begging … the weakest tenet connection in the article — it adds nothing substantive", and the objection is unanswered after ~6 months: MWI predicts branching is **locally undetectable**, so altered states feeling like "different modes of *this* consciousness" is equally what MWI predicts. The sentence asserts consistency, which is true and empty. **What makes it conspicuous is the company it keeps** — the three neighbouring tenet paragraphs are scrupulous ("silent on it" for MQI; "preferred by fit rather than forced by the evidence" for Tenet 3; "without by itself establishing dualism" for Tenet 1), and Tenet 4 alone gets an unhedged appeal to phenomenology. ### ✓ 2026-09-10: `concepts/altered-states-of-consciousness` — six sourcing-fidelity defects, incl. the Xu 2023 near-death evidence stripped of the seizure confound its own authors flag, and an unattributed quotation whose provenance chain is broken at the research note
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/altered-states-of-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: Full reasoning and every verification transcript in `obsidian/reviews/pessimistic-2026-09-10-altered-states-of-consciousness.md`. Line numbers are from this run — **re-read from disk before editing**. **⚠️ LENGTH: 3,396 words, `soft_warning`, concepts hard threshold 3,500 — 104 words of headroom** (measured with `tools.curate.length.analyze_length`, which is **already body-only**; do not subtract frontmatter from it). This bundle is designed to be **near word-neutral**, and **step (0) must run first because it funds the rest**. **(0) DELETE TWO DANGLING REFERENCES — do this first, ~−20 words.** `James, W. (1902). *The Varieties of Religious Experience*` and `Kelly, E. F. et al. (2007). *Irreducible Mind*` sit in the References list and **have never been cited in prose**. Verified against the creation commit `8471af2701`: both appear in that commit only inside the References section, and a prose search of the current file for `James` and `Kelly` returns −1 for each. **(1) PRIMARY — L117 §Near-Death Experiences, restore the confound the cited paper states. ~+25 words, HIGH severity.** Article: "Xu et al. (2023) found gamma surges in dying patients". Verified at full text (Europe PMC `PMC10175832` `fullTextXML`; positive controls 172× "gamma", 55× "patient", 13× "hot zone" — the PMC HTML page is CAPTCHA-walled and returns a **false zero**, use the XML endpoint): the cohort was **four comatose patients**, **two of four** showed the surge, and the paper states verbatim: *"Heightened gamma activities at near-death were detected in two of the four patients in our study. Interestingly, both of these patients had a history of seizures: one (Pt1) experienced seizure only during her pregnancies, while the other (Pt3) developed status epilepticus a day prior to her terminal cardiac arrest."* It then raises pathological high-frequency oscillations as a candidate reading. The article omits the n, the split and the confound while asserting the paper's supportive framing — and one paragraph later at L119 writes "Sceptics note the lack of falsifiable predictions", so the sceptic's actual best objection is inside the article's own citation and off the page. Install n + split + seizure history in one clause. **⚠️ DO NOT "FIX" THE TPO / OUT-OF-BODY ATTRIBUTION AT THE SAME LOCUS — IT IS CORRECT.** I suspected it and disproved my own suspicion at full text: the paper says *"the TPO junctions are considered a 'hot zone' for the neural correlates of consciousness"* and *"stimulation of the right TP junction (TPj) in epilepsy patients reliably induced OBE"*. None of this is in the abstract, so a reviewer who stops at the abstract will manufacture a false defect here. **(2) L103 §Meditation — unattributed, non-verbatim quotation. HIGH severity, word-neutral fix.** `"radically altered states characterised by full loss of consciousness"` is presented as verbatim and carries **no author, no year and no reference entry** — the only such quotation in the article. Provenance: `obsidian/research/altered-states-consciousness-2026-01-19.md` L130/L135 renders it *"Cessations are radically altered states characterized by a full loss of consciousness…"* and attributes it to `PMC9493263`. That article is "Reducing and deducing the structures of consciousness through meditation" (Tantric Yoga); fetched in full with positive controls (177× "meditat", 40× "Tantric", 211× "consciousness"), the word **"cessation" occurs once**, in an unrelated footnote about *moksha*/*nirvana*. So the note's attribution is wrong and the article inherited the quote having dropped even that. The article's string is also not verbatim against its own upstream transcription: `a full loss` → `full loss`, and `characterized` → `characterised` **inside the quotation marks**. **PREFERRED FIX: de-quote to paraphrase — word-neutral and closes the issue.** The likely true source is Laukkonen et al. (2023) *Progress in Brain Research* (DOI `10.1016/bs.pbr.2022.12.007`), but this is **UNCONFIRMED** — the chapter is not open access, OpenAlex has no abstract for it, and a Europe PMC exact-phrase search returned 0, which is **not** proof of absence for a non-OA book chapter. **Do not cite Laukkonen without verifying at the chapter text, and do not assert fabrication.** **(3) L51 — Lu et al. (2025) over-read. Word-neutral or negative.** "…—neuroimaging confirmation that ASCs represent discrete modes rather than continuous variation." The **five nuclei** figure is correct and verified (*"Five key nuclei demonstrated state-specific alterations: two first-order (VLp, ventral posterolateral) and three higher-order nuclei (pulvinar, centromedian, mediodorsal)"*), as is all the metadata. The **conclusion** is not: the same abstract reports *"Decreased complexity/variability occurred in 4-6 nuclei during anesthesia and 4-5 in DoC patients"* and that *"Nucleus-specific coupling … systematically varied with consciousness state"*, across a sleep arm of graded stages (wakefulness / NREM1 / NREM2). A connectivity study reporting systematic variation across graded states does not adjudicate discrete-vs-continuous. Fix: "neuroimaging confirmation that" → "consistent with"; drop or soften the discrete/continuous contrast. **(4) L73 — the March finding was answered with more of the excluded support, uncited.** `pessimistic-2026-03-20.md` flagged "some psychedelic insights prove verifiable (therapeutic breakthroughs, creative solutions)" as needing citations because **therapeutic benefit ≠ insight verification**. The claim is retained verbatim and the article **appended** "Clinical studies of psilocybin-assisted therapy show lasting improvements in depression and anxiety that correlate with reported mystical-type experiences, suggesting the insights have functional validity beyond subjective impression" — precisely the excluded category, **uncited** (no author, no year, no reference entry), and "functional validity" is still not epistemic verification. "creative solutions" has no support of any kind. **Cheapest correct fix is the downgrade** (claim therapeutic outcome, not insight verification) — word-neutral to negative. If instead citing, the 2023-2025 record includes substantial work on blinding failure and expectancy, so an uncaveated version would be the pre-2022 framing. **(5) L105 — attribute a superlative. ~+6 words.** "the most rigorous test to date—Kral et al. (2022)" is the **authors' own** self-description (*"the largest and most rigorously controlled study to date"*) stated as the Map's finding. Everything else in that sentence verifies exactly: n = 218 ✓, active controls ✓, null structural result ✓. Attribute or drop the superlative. **(6) References — two alphabetisation defects, word-neutral.** Fuchs (2005) sits before Csikszentmihalyi (1990); Lu (2025) sits after Tart (1969) at the end of the list. The rest of the list is strictly alphabetical and unnumbered, so no cross-reference renumbering risk applies. **⚠️ DO NOT RE-LITIGATE THESE — VERIFIED CLEAN AT SOURCE THIS RUN**: the Revonsuo quote *"increased tendency to produce misrepresentations"* is **verbatim** in the paper's abstract with correct metadata (OpenAlex `mag` 2053357193, so the record predates 2022 and contamination is ruled out); Xu/Lu/Kral/Mashour/Kerskens/Carhart-Harris & Friston/Dietrich metadata all ✓; the three `tenets#^…` anchor fragments all resolve. **Jiang et al. (2017) Cereb. Cortex 27(8), 4083-4093 is CORRECT** — Crossref shows 2016 with no issue because that is the advance-access record (DOI `10.1093/cercor/bhw220`); do not "fix" it to 2016. **⚠️ The altered-state symmetry audit PASSES** (`evaluate_symmetry` → `None`; supportive 5, disruptive 2, 6 markers) and this file's **L77 is the corpus exemplar** cited by the open 2026-09-08 `arguments-against-materialism` entry — **do not install a symmetry section, do not pad toward the marker vocabulary, and do not weaken the production-theorist accommodations at L77 / L95 / L113 / L155 in any length pass.** Sync both trees and grep-verify each edit landed in `hugo/content/` as well as `obsidian/`. Pileup checked at mint: 0 other open tasks target this file.

- **Type**: refine-draft

- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-10 (Gemini 2.5 Pro); read that file's `## Verification Notes` first — the review fabricates several quotes it attributes to the Map, including "actualizes one element of the improper mixture into a definite experience" (zero occurrences anywhere in either tree). **A fabricated quote does not refute the gap**, and this gap was checked separately and is real. **THE FINDING, verified at mint:** `concepts/improper-vs-proper-mixtures` has **zero** occurrences of `wigner`, `bong`, or `local friendliness` (case-insensitive, both trees). The structural problem: the Map's selection story needs the selected outcome to be an *absolute* fact (not merely relative to the selecting subject), and it also claims to operate inside unitary quantum mechanics without breaking it. Bong et al. prove those two commitments are jointly incompatible with the empirical predictions of QM once observers can themselves be treated as quantum systems — the conjunction of Absoluteness of Observed Events, Locality, and No-Superdeterminism yields inequalities QM violates. If selection makes the outcome absolute, Wigner cannot recohere the friend's lab and the Map has an objective collapse after all (which its own timing arguments elsewhere try to avoid); if the outcome is only relative to the selector, the position slides toward Relational QM or Everett, both of which the Map declines. **The Map should say which horn it takes.** It is entitled to take one — the honest answer may well be that it accepts a detectable-in-principle departure from unitarity — but the choice is currently unmade and unstated. **VERIFIED CITATIONS** (Crossref-confirmed at mint, safe to cite): Bong, Utreras-Alarcón, Ghafari, Liang, Tischler, Cavalcanti, Pryde & Wiseman (2020), "A strong no-go theorem on the Wigner's friend paradox", *Nature Physics* 16, DOI `10.1038/s41567-020-0990-x`; and Wiseman, Cavalcanti & Rieffel (2023), "A 'thoughtful' Local Friendliness no-go theorem", *Quantum*, DOI `10.22331/q-2023-09-14-1112` — the second is directly on point for the Map because it treats *having thoughts* as sufficient for observerhood. **DO NOT** cite the review's "Moreno et al. 2022" or "Pipa 2025" for the RQM half — neither resolved to a real record at mint. **THE FIX:** a short subsection in `concepts/post-decoherence-selection` naming the dilemma and stating which horn the Map takes, plus a piped wikilink from `concepts/improper-vs-proper-mixtures` (zero word cost) so the two articles do not answer the question differently. Check `concepts/von-neumann-wigner-interpretation` and `topics/born-rule-and-the-consciousness-interface` before writing — the same-day Claude review claimed related framing lives there, and that claim was NOT verified. **LENGTH:** `post-decoherence-selection` measured 2336 words, `ok`, 164 below the 2500 concepts soft threshold — budget is real but not large; re-measure before editing. Sync both trees and grep-verify.

Its argument that an improper mixture is a gap requiring a selecting principle only goes through if the global state is *pure* — i.e. it silently assumes Wave Function Realism. If the fundamental state of the universe can itself be an impure density matrix, the ontological boundary between proper and improper mixtures does not hold at the fundamental level, and the subsystem's mixed state is exactly as real, and of the same mathematical class, as the universal state. That is a premise the article uses without ever naming or defending. **This is NOT the same finding as the Bohmian/hidden-variable horn** in the sibling P1 task on this file — that one adds a route that closes the outcome gap; this one attacks the purity premise the gap is defined against. Roderich Tumulka is the standard co-developer of the density-matrix-realist formulations; **verify any Tumulka-specific claim at the publisher before asserting it**, it was not checked at mint. **DO NOT** repeat the review's looser gloss that the Wentaculus was "published in BJPS *and* Oxford University Press" — that runs a paper and a book together. **THE FIX:** name the assumption and either defend it or mark it as an assumption. Two or three sentences in the section that sets up the gap is enough; the honest version concedes that the argument is conditional on the global state being pure, which is a live and contested question, rather than pretending the improper/proper boundary is interpretation-neutral. Effect is adverse to the Map — do not let the edit read as a strengthening. **⚠ SHARED LENGTH BUDGET.** Measured at mint with `tools.curate.length.analyze_length` (already body-only — do not subtract frontmatter): **2556 words, `soft_warning`, 56 words PAST the 2500 concepts soft threshold and 944 below the 3500 hard threshold.** The sibling P1 task on this same file budgets ~250 words for its three fixes. ### ✓ 2026-09-10: two Zheng & Meister bandwidth-figure defects surviving the 2026-09-10 relabel sweep — one wrong ratio, one wrong source attribution

Both are distinct from the label defect that sweep fixed, and neither was touched by it. **(1) ARITHMETIC — `concepts/consciousness-selecting-neural-patterns` L78**: "conscious thought operates at approximately 10 bits per second-five million times slower than unconscious sensory processing (~10 to the 9 bits/s)". The ratio is 10 to the 9 over 10 = **10 to the 8, one hundred million**, which is Zheng & Meister's own sifting number (their Equation 2). "Five million" is wrong by a factor of 20. The *label* here ("unconscious sensory processing") is acceptable, so this is purely the number. **(2) SOURCE ATTRIBUTION — `concepts/interface-friction` L52**: "the brain handles on the order of 10 to the 9 bits per second (Zimmermann 1986; Norretranders 1998)". Those two sources give **~11 million** bits/s of sensory transduction, not 10 to the 9; the 10 to the 9 figure is Zheng & Meister's one-eye cone-population capacity (~1.6 Gbit/s). So the magnitude is attributed to sources that do not support it. Same line also spells **Norretranders** without the slashed o, twice in that file, against the corpus-canonical `Norretranders` with the slashed o. **MODEL FOR BOTH FIXES**: `voids/resolution-void.md` L48, which states the distinction correctly and is the corpus's ratified wording; `topics/bandwidth-of-consciousness` L67/L107 were brought into line on 2026-09-10. **DO NOT** re-open the eight files fixed in that sweep. **Measure length before editing** - not measured for either file this run.

### ✓ 2026-09-10: `topics/bandwidth-of-consciousness` L67 attributes Zheng & Meister's ~10⁹ bit/s to "aggregate neural processing" — it is their SENSORY/peripheral rate, which mislabels the sifting number's numerator
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/bandwidth-of-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: **Found by the fork repairing `voids/resolution-void` on 2026-09-09 and reported rather than actioned (its target was that file only); verified independently by the driver against the published source.** ⚠️ This matters more than a lone mislabel because `resolution-void` was just harmonised **against this article as the model** (commit `72ab4b74`) — so the corrected article now points at an uncorrected one. **THE LOCUS — L67, verbatim**: "Sensory transduction delivers roughly 11 million bits per second to the brain (Zimmermann 1986; Nørretranders 1998), and **aggregate neural processing runs at ~10⁹ bits per second (Zheng & Meister 2025)**. Yet conscious influence on behaviour measures at approximately 10 bits per second. Zheng and Meister call this *ratio* the \"sifting number\"—roughly **100 million to one against total neural processing**, and a million to one against sensory transduction." **WHY IT IS WRONG, from the source.** The published *Neuron* Perspective (Zheng & Meister 2025, DOI `10.1016/j.neuron.2024.11.008`; free PDF on the Meister lab site; preprint arXiv **2408.10234**, wording identical) defines the quantity in **Equation 2**: `S_i = Sifting Number = Sensory information rate / Behavioral throughput ≈ 1 Gbit/s / 10 bit/s = 10⁸`. Its ~1 Gbit/s is explicitly **peripheral/sensory**, derived in the same passage: "one human cone photoreceptor can transmit information at ≈270 bits/s. The population of **6 million cones in one eye** has a capacity of about **1.6 gigabits/s**." So (a) ~10⁹ is **not** "aggregate neural processing", and (b) the sifting number is **not** "against total neural processing" — its numerator is the sensory rate. **FIX**: relabel ~10⁹ as the peripheral/sensory capacity and restate the sifting number as sensory-rate-over-behavioural-throughput. **WHAT THIS ARTICLE ALREADY GETS RIGHT — do not disturb**: it sources the 11-million figure correctly to `Zimmermann 1986; Nørretranders 1998` (the corpus-canonical form, and the year is **1986** — a driver guess of 1989 was wrong), and it reports **both** ratios rather than one, which is why it was the model for `resolution-void`'s repair. ⚠️ Note `10⁹` occurs **4** times in this file — check each; only the sifting-number context is at issue. ⚠️ Minor, same sentence: "conscious influence on behaviour **measures at**" mildly asserts measurement of what is a review's synthesised estimate; "is put at" is safer. ⚠️ Measure length before editing; not measured this run. Sync both trees.

Europe PMC `pubTypeList` for DOI `10.1016/j.neuron.2024.11.008` (PMID 39694032) is `[research-article, **Review**, Journal Article]`, and the abstract's own framing is explanatory and programmatic — "we propose new research directions to remedy this" — i.e. it synthesises existing throughput estimates rather than reporting a new measurement. **FIX**: "put the figure at" / "estimate" / "survey the evidence for". ⚠️ The second `measured` in the same section — "Wu et al. (2016) measured deliberate selection…" — is a **different** claim about a **different** paper and was **not** checked; do not sweep both on one verdict. **(2) VERIFIED — an order-of-magnitude mismatch with the cited source.** The paper's abstract states sensory systems "gather data at ∼10⁹ bits/s", so *its* contrast with 10 bits/s is **10⁸ — eight orders**. The article's section is titled "**Six Orders of Magnitude**" and its "Consciousness discards 99.9999%" both derive from the **eleven million** figure (11×10⁶/10 ≈ 10⁶), yet the same paragraph credits Zheng & Meister with characterising "the ratio between sensory processing and conscious throughput". The article does separately note "The brain itself operates at an estimated 10⁹ bits per second", so the two numbers coexist unreconciled: **the ratio is computed from one source and attributed to another.** **(3) VERIFIED — the headline figure is unattributed.** "eleven million bits per second" appears at **L3 (description)**, **L34 (body lead)** and in the Six Orders section, and the article gives **no source for it anywhere**. It is the classic sensory-receptor bandwidth estimate (Zimmermann 1989, popularised by Nørretranders) — cite it or drop it, but do not leave the article's lead number sourceless. **(4) NOT VERIFIED, do not treat as fabricated.** The section attributes a quoted phrase to the paper: "the largest unexplained number in brain science". The paper is **paywalled** (`isOpenAccess: N`); Europe PMC serves no full-text XML and the PMC landing page is an 11KB stub in which even the benign phrase "reading, typing" returns −1, so **absence there proves nothing**. Someone with journal access must check the exact wording before it is altered or defended. ⚠️ Length: 2,051 words, `voids` thresholds (2000, 3000, 4000) — `soft_warning`, 949 below hard, so there is room to add a citation line. Sync both trees.

### ✓ 2026-09-09: `concepts/global-workspace-theory` cites the Dehaene-Naccache commentary for its endorsement and withholds that the same document calls the article's own conclusion "a residual crypto-dualism" — plus a misreported Nanda verdict and a conceded confound spent twice
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/global-workspace-theory.md
- **Notes**: From pessimistic review 2026-09-09; full evidence and verbatim source extracts in [pessimistic-2026-09-09-global-workspace-theory](/reviews/pessimistic-2026-09-09-global-workspace-theory/). Re-read from disk before editing; line numbers below are from this run. LENGTH IS COMFORTABLE: 3048 words against concepts thresholds (2500 soft / 3500 hard), so 452 below hard — and items 1-3 replace text rather than add, so the net cost is roughly the two or three sentences item 1 needs. Do not treat this as a condense. **(1) PRIMARY, the J-space section (L106-112) — the cited commentary is a hostile witness on the decisive point and the article does not say so.** L108 reports that Dehaene and Naccache "were invited as commentators and read J-space as a genuine computational workspace rather than a loose analogy". Both halves are TRUE and verified this run against the primary artefact (the external-commentary PDF at Anthropic's CDN, extracted and read in full; it opens "we invited commentary from several experts", and they call the finding "a landmark in consciousness research"). So do NOT weaken or remove that sentence. The defect is what comes next: L112 concludes the phenomenal question "stays exactly where dualism locates it—untouched" and that GWT gains its mechanism "without the hard problem moving an inch" — while the SAME commentary explicitly forecloses that inference, in a passage that anticipates the objection by name: "Critiques will undoubtedly object that none of this work touches upon phenomenal consciousness... We and others, however, have argued that this supposedly 'hard problem of consciousness' will dissipate once we clarify in sufficient detail the supposedly 'easy problem' of how conscious information is processed. Ill-defined intuitions of 'qualia', 'subjective phenomenal experience' and 'what it is like', when pushed hard, often disclose a residual crypto-dualism or vitalism". FIX: two to three sentences in the J-space section acknowledging that the GNW originators draw the opposite conclusion from the same result. The Map need not win the exchange in the article, only show it. This STRENGTHENS the section — "the theory's own originators think this result bears on the phenomenal question" is a more interesting fact than "the authors declined to comment". **(2) SAME SECTION, L112 — an abstention is doing metaphysical work.** "For the Map, the decisive feature is what the authors decline to claim" is fine and the verbatim Anthropic caveat quoted just above it is EXACT (checked word for word against the paper; do not touch it). But authorial silence about the phenomenal question is not evidence that the question is unaffected. Split the readings: keep "this result does not itself settle the phenomenal question, and its authors say so"; drop or argue for "untouched" and "moving an inch". Items 1 and 2 are one pass — the commentary material in item 1 is the bridging argument this claim currently lacks. **(3) SAME SECTION, L110 — Nanda's abstention is reported as a critical verdict.** The article says he judged the mechanism real and safety-useful "while cautioning that the consciousness framing is its weakest leg". The replication and safety-usefulness are accurate (verified: "We have replicated the core claims on Qwen 3.6 27B"). The third clause is not. Verbatim from his commentary: "I won't express a strong opinion on the philosophical claim - I do not feel qualified to assess whether this is really analogous to a global workspace, and this feels like the least interesting claim to me." He DECLINES to judge and calls it least interesting; the article renders that as a negative verdict, in the direction that favours the Map. What he does offer is narrower and more useful: "it's easy to read too much into post-hoc analysis of results like this." FIX: report what he said — persuaded the cognitive space is real, declines to assess the workspace analogy, warns against over-reading. Also note he calls J-Lens "useful (but limited)". **(4) L96 and L166 — the hedge granted at L84-88 does not travel.** The article concedes at L84 that weak prefrontal decoding may be methodological (prefrontal cortex "lacks the columnar organisation that makes posterior decoding tractable") and declines to adjudicate; L88 respects this ("consistent with the underdetermination framing above rather than a clean falsification"). But L96 ("if conscious content tracks posterior cortex rather than prefrontal networks, neither broadcast, higher-order representation, nor attention schemas may be necessary") and L166 ("COGITATE suggests broadcast may not be necessary") both rest on the prefrontal half the article agreed might be an artefact. FIX: carry the qualification to both loci — a clause each is enough. The dreaming evidence at L88 is INDEPENDENT of the decoding confound and can carry more of the load. **(5) L158-162 §The Simplicity Objection — an in-framework objection answered from outside it.** The parsimony charge is raised in the opponent's register and answered with "the [Occam's Razor Has Limits] tenet responds", which gives a GWT proponent no reason to move. The in-framework reply is already half-present in the next clause and needs no tenet: GWT's simplicity is bought by narrowing the explanandum, so the comparison is not like-for-like, and parsimony only adjudicates between theories with the same explanandum. FIX: lead with that in natural prose; let the tenet follow as the Map's framing rather than as the response. Keep all editor vocabulary out of the article text. **(6) L164-170 — an internal contradiction inside one section.** L168 says "Current evidence leans toward dissociability"; L170 says the impasse "may be constitutive rather than merely temporary". A constitutive impasse means no evidence can lean, which voids L168 — and it volunteers in-principle unfalsifiability against the Map's own interest, under a heading that promises an answer about testability. FIX: the defensible narrower line is that the CURRENT methodological repertoire cannot distinguish the readings because every detection route runs through access, while leaving open whether that is permanent. **(7) MINOR, low priority, only if the pass has room.** (a) L102 gives Block's Chinese-nation thought experiment unattributed; Block is in the references but for the 2007 overflow paper, not the 1978 functionalism argument — four words would settle it. (b) L118 calls Streicher et al. (2025) a "meta-analysis"; it is a reanalysis of 80 conditions from 16 fMRI studies with no pooled effect-size estimation. The 10% figure IS correct (verified at the publisher: "only eight of the 80 experimental conditions (10%)") — change only the study-type label. (c) L160 attributes a verbatim quote to "researchers acknowledge"; the wording is the SEP entry *The Neuroscience of Consciousness* summarising Block 2007, and the quote including "in report" is EXACT — verified in raw page text, so do not alter the quote, only name its source. (d) L118's activity-silent working-memory claim carries no citation. **CLEARED THIS RUN — do not spend time re-checking:** the COGITATE citation form is canonical as written (Consortium byline, not Melloni-first) and must NOT be changed; the 2026 Correction to the Naccache rejoinder alters only a fourth author's name the article never mentions; the J-space capacity figures (k up to 25, never more than 10% of variance) match the paper; the third disanalogy about attention carrying working memory IS supported by the paper's own text; the altered-state symmetry audit does not apply (zero supportive-cluster items, word-bounded grep with a positive control); no editor-vocabulary label leakage; all 34 wikilink targets resolve uniquely. Lines 70-71 carry **all three** defects corrected in the article this cycle: (1) "locomote (driven by heart-cell contraction), **navigate, push payloads**, and self-repair after damage" — `payload` occurs **exactly once** in Kriegman et al. 2020 (Europe PMC `PMC6994979`) and not as a result ("passive tissue, which provides space for *future organ systems or payloads*"); the observed behaviour is aggregation of loose objects and debris. `navigate` greps **0** in that paper; navigation belongs to Blackiston et al. 2021, *Science Robotics* 6(52), eabf1571, `10.1126/scirobotics.abf1571`. The platform story is not "2020 sculpted / 2021 sculpted". **SCOPE: this file only** — a corpus-wide sweep across `obsidian/` and `archive/` returns this as the single remaining content locus for any of the three strings; every other hit is a review, changelog or todo entry quoting the defect as the thing being fixed, and those are correctly left alone. **BUDGET: comfortable** — check with `analyze_length`; `research` thresholds are 2500/3500/5000. **NOTE THE CLASS OF FILE**: this is a research note, not an article — the fix is to make the note's summary of the source accurate, not to rewrite it as prose. Claude's citation table calls Kriegman et al. 2020 metadata-exact and its description — "evolutionary algorithm designs body plans in silico; built from *Xenopus laevis* skin and cardiac cells; locomotion driven by rhythmic cardiac contraction; navigation, payload-pushing, self-repair" — "faithful", which directly ratifies the two phrases ChatGPT charges. It is NOT adjudicated here, but two asymmetries bear on it: ChatGPT's charge is source-specific and names an alternative source of record (Blackiston et al. 2021, 10.1126/scirobotics.abf1571, for the self-organised ciliated platform), whereas Claude's is a summary judgement with no corresponding entry in its own verification notes. Specifically: "spontaneously cooperate to build" is false for the 2020 platform, whose forms were surgically shaped — spontaneous self-organisation belongs to Blackiston 2021; "push payloads" is unsupported by the 2020 experimental result, where the observed behaviour is aggregating loose particles/debris; "none of this is behaviour the source cells exhibit" is false as an ABSOLUTE (the reviewer's point is that the novelty is organism-scale coordination of inherited cellular capacities, not wholly new capacities); "navigate" is underspecified; and kinematic replication needs its operational qualification (supplied dissociated cells, fabricated parent forms, controlled environment) while retaining the term in its specific kinematic sense. SUGGESTED FIX: split the xenobot passage so construction method and demonstrated capacities are stated per platform. VERIFIED ON DISK: the article says the programme's cognition is "explicitly agnostic about, and not asserting, felt experience" (offset 9418, scoped to TAME, the light-cone paper and the Dennett collaboration) and that panpsychist readings "are not Levin's own careful bracketing" (offset 13820). VERIFIED AT CROSSREF this run — the papers the reviewer cites against those claims are real, with correct metadata: Rouleau & Levin, "The Multiple Realizability of Sentience in Living Systems and Beyond", *eNeuro* 10(11), 10.1523/ENEURO.0375-23.2023; Fields, Glazebrook & Levin, "Minimal physicalism as a scale-free substrate for cognition and consciousness", *Neuroscience of Consciousness* 2021(2), 10.1093/nc/niab013. Levin co-authoring a positive multiple-realizability-of-sentience paper is hard to square with a *programme-wide* agnosticism attribution. NOTE THE SCOPE CAREFULLY — do not over-correct: the article's sentence is scoped to three specific works, and for those particular experimental presentations the bracketing claim may well be accurate. What fails is the generalisation from those works to "the programme", and the flat denial that continuity/panpsychist-adjacent readings are Levin's own. INTERNAL CONTRADICTION LEG (reviewer §3.4): the sibling `topics/synthetic-minimal-agents-and-the-engineered-decoupling` already identifies Rouleau & Levin's sentience paper as the strongest rival position — so two Map articles currently disagree about what Levin holds. SUGGESTED FIX: state that particular experimental presentations bracket first-person experience while Levin's 2019/2021/2022 work advances positive, speculative continuity hypotheses; present that graded view directly and then give the Map's reasons for rejecting it, rather than denying it is his. Sync both trees.

### ✓ 2026-09-09: `topics/basal-and-bioelectric-cognition` slides from "not sufficient" to "no evidence either way", contradicting the Map's own [P-CS2](/positions/consciousness-scope/#p-cs2)
- **Type**: refine-draft

The article's clause that single cells and voltage gradients are what "even Levin declines to characterise as felt" is also the support for its reductio ("otherwise it marches all the way down to single cells and voltage gradients"), and that reductio is the only thing standing between "agency is no evidence either way" and "the boundary must accordingly be weighted toward phenomenal markers". Correcting the Levin stance therefore strands the reductio and re-opens the non-sequitur. Claude's verdict, verbatim: "The two P1s must be fixed together, not separately." Convergent: ChatGPT §2 — "the current argument gains much of its apparent force from an equivocation between **not proving**, **not warranting on its own**, and **providing no evidence**." Claude §2(b), reached independently and named an "epistemic-to-metaphysical slide" — "'The boundary of consciousness must accordingly be weighted toward phenomenal markers over bare competency markers' does not follow from 'agency is no evidence either way.' If competency is genuinely *neutral* evidence, it cannot *mandate* reweighting; it can only fail to move the needle." Priority unchanged: already P1, the synthesis ceiling. ORIGINAL PER-REVIEW NOTES FOLLOW. From outer review 2026-09-09 (ChatGPT 5.6 Pro), §2 and improvements 1 and 15 — the review's headline finding. VERIFIED ON DISK this run: the article reads "the point is only that their agency is no evidence either way" (offset 13763). The reviewer distinguishes three propositions the article runs together: (1) agency does not *entail* consciousness — true; (2) agency is *insufficient on its own* to warrant attribution — plausible and well supported by the cases; (3) agency is *evidentially neutral* — much stronger, requiring a likelihood ratio P(A|C)/P(A|¬C) ≈ 1 that nothing in the planarian or xenobot work estimates. The article's own good qualifications (experience not shown absent, markers defeasible, functionalist readings allowed) coexist with (3), so this is an INTERNAL inconsistency, not an external disagreement — the reviewer explicitly pre-refuted the unfair charges in its §1 and confirms the article does *not* claim these organisms are certainly unconscious. SECOND, HARDER LEG: the Map's own register contradicts (3). `positions/consciousness-scope` [P-CS2](/positions/consciousness-scope/#p-cs2) says behavioural sophistication transfers "no verdict on its own" (verified at offset 10518 in `obsidian/positions/consciousness-scope.md`) — which is proposition (2), not (3). The article's positions block cites only [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1) (offset 11437); a grep for `P-CS2` in the article returns **-1, absent**. SUGGESTED FIX: adopt the reviewer's replacement — agency "does not entail consciousness, supplies no verdict on its own, and has an evidential weight that depends on substrate, homology and convergence with other markers" — and link the article's central conclusion to [P-CS2](/positions/consciousness-scope/#p-cs2), leaving [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1) as a downstream implication rather than the only registered position. Check the resulting prose against the article's length budget before adding. Sync both trees.
